+class PortAllocationBlocked(RuntimeError):
+    """Lease allocation refused; ``findings`` carries the allocator's reasons."""
+
+    def __init__(self, message: str, findings: Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]] = None) -> None:
+        super().__init__(message)
+        self.findings: List[Dict[str, Any]] = list(findings or [])
+
+
+def _port_vars_for(name: str, catalog: Mapping[str, Any]) -> List[str]:
+    spec = (catalog.get("servers") or {}).get(name) or {}
+    if not isinstance(spec, dict):
+        return []
+    out = [spec.get("port_var")] + [
+        e.get("var") for e in (spec.get("extra_port_vars") or []) if isinstance(e, dict)
+    ]
+    return [str(v) for v in out if v]
+
+
+def _leased_ports(
+    lease_reg: PortLeaseRegistry,
+    identity: ProjectIdentityView,
+    wanted: Sequence[str],
+) -> Dict[str, int]:
+    """Active leases that belong to this project/worktree, keyed by port env var."""
+    found: Dict[str, int] = {}
+    for lease in lease_reg.active_leases():
+        var = lease.get("port_var")
+        if var not in wanted or not lease.get("port"):
+            continue
+        if lease_reg.identity_matches(
+            lease,
+            worktree_root=identity.worktree_root,
+            project_root=identity.project_root,
+            worktree_hash=identity.worktree_hash,
+            project_id=identity.project_id,
+        ):
+            found[str(var)] = int(lease["port"])
+    return found
+
+
+def _service_for_port_var(var: str, catalog: Mapping[str, Any]) -> Optional[str]:
+    for name in (catalog.get("servers") or {}):
+        if var in _port_vars_for(str(name), catalog):
+            return str(name)
+    return None
+
+
+def _leases_held_by_other_service(
+    leased: Mapping[str, int],
+    catalog: Mapping[str, Any],
+    docker: Optional[di.DockerInspectResult],
+    project_id: Optional[str],
+) -> Dict[str, str]:
+    """``{port_var: owner}`` for leased ports published by another service's container.
+
+    Same condition as the ``DOCKER_CONTAINER_PORT_COLLISION`` service-label check
+    in ``_collision_checks``: managed, this ``dopemux.project_id``, and a
+    ``dopemux.service`` that is not the service the port variable belongs to.
+    """
+    if docker is None or not docker.available or not project_id:
+        return {}
+    owners: Dict[int, di.DockerContainerInfo] = {}
+    for c in docker.containers:
+        for published in c.published_ports or []:
+            owners[int(published)] = c
+    held: Dict[str, str] = {}
+    for var, port in leased.items():
+        owner = owners.get(int(port))
+        if owner is None:
+            continue
+        labels = owner.labels or {}
+        service = _service_for_port_var(var, catalog)
+        owner_service = labels.get("dopemux.service") or ""
+        if (
+            labels.get(dr.LABEL_MANAGED) == "true"
+            and labels.get("dopemux.project_id") == project_id
+            and service
+            and owner_service
+            and owner_service != service
+        ):
+            held[var] = f"{owner.name} (service={owner_service})"
+    return held
+
+
+def _resolve_ports(
+    identity: ProjectIdentityView,
+    selected: Sequence[str],
+    catalog: Mapping[str, Any],
+    *,
+    lease_path: Path,
+    source: str = "dopemux mcp start",
+    env: Optional[Mapping[str, str]] = None,
+    allocate: bool = True,
+    is_free_fn: Optional[Callable[[int], bool]] = None,
+    docker: Optional[di.DockerInspectResult] = None,
+) -> Dict[str, int]:
+    """Port map ``{port_env_var: port}`` for ``selected`` services.
+
+    Order: ports an envrc/process env already names win; otherwise the active
+    leases bound to this project/worktree; otherwise (when ``allocate``)
+    allocate and persist new leases under ``registry_lock``. The lock is held
+    only for the release/allocate-and-save step. Task Orchestrator is a reserved
+    singleton port, never leased, and is left to the caller's default.
+
+    With ``docker`` and ``allocate``, a leased port that a same-project container
+    of another service publishes (a cross-service collision an older
+    one-service-at-a-time allocation could write) is released and re-allocated
+    instead of failing the start.
+    """
+    ports = _extract_ports(env or {}, selected, catalog)
+    lease_services = [
+        n
+        for n in selected
+        if n != "task-orchestrator" and any(v not in ports for v in _port_vars_for(n, catalog))
+    ]
+    if not lease_services or not identity.worktree_root or not identity.project_root:
+        return ports
+
+    wanted = [v for n in lease_services for v in _port_vars_for(n, catalog)]
+    current = _leased_ports(PortLeaseRegistry.load(lease_path), identity, wanted)
+    leased = {var: port for var, port in current.items() if var not in ports}
+    stale = _leases_held_by_other_service(leased, catalog, docker, identity.project_id)
+    if not allocate:
+        stale = {}
+    for var, port in leased.items():
+        if var not in stale:
+            ports.setdefault(var, port)
+    lease_services = [
+        n for n in lease_services if any(v not in ports for v in _port_vars_for(n, catalog))
+    ]
+    if not lease_services or not allocate:
+        return ports
+
+    from .port_allocator import allocate_ports
+
+    # Exactly the call `mcp_commands._allocate_ports` makes (allocate_ports_map):
+    # no `identity=`, so the allocator derives the same lease identity as `init`.
+    with registry_lock(lease_path):
+        if stale:
+            leases = PortLeaseRegistry.load(lease_path)
+            for lease in leases.active_leases():
+                var = str(lease.get("port_var") or "")
+                if (
+                    var in stale
+                    and int(lease.get("port") or 0) == leased[var]
+                    and leases.identity_matches(
+                        lease,
+                        worktree_root=identity.worktree_root,
+                        project_root=identity.project_root,
+                        worktree_hash=identity.worktree_hash,
+                        project_id=identity.project_id,
+                    )
+                ):
+                    leases.mark_released(lease_id=str(lease.get("lease_id")))
+            leases.save()
+        result = allocate_ports(
+            lease_services,
+            catalog,
+            worktree=identity.worktree_root,
+            project_root=identity.project_root,
+            existing_envrc=env,
+            registry_path=lease_path,
+            persist=True,
+            dry_run=False,
+            source=source,
+            is_free_fn=is_free_fn,
+        )
+    if result.status == "BLOCKED":
+        msgs = "; ".join(f["message"] for f in result.blocking_findings if f.get("message"))
+        raise PortAllocationBlocked(
+            msgs or "Port allocation blocked", result.blocking_findings
+        )
+    for var, port in result.ports.items():
+        ports.setdefault(str(var), int(port))
+    return ports
+
+
+def runtime_dir_for_paths(
+    *, project_root: str | Path, worktree_hash: str, registry_path: Optional[Path] = None
+) -> Path:
+    """The one rule for a worktree's runtime dir: ``<registry dir>/<slug>-<worktree_hash>``."""
+    slug = dr.project_slug(Path(project_root).name)
+    base = registry_path or default_registry_path()
+    return base.parent / f"{slug}-{worktree_hash}"
+
+
+def runtime_dir_for(
+    identity: ProjectIdentityView, *, registry_path: Optional[Path] = None
+) -> Path:
+    """Per-worktree runtime dir: ``<registry dir>/<slug>-<worktree_hash>``.
+
+    Shared by the start path, the launcher and reap so they agree on one path.
+    """
+    worktree_root = identity.worktree_root
+    project_root = identity.project_root or worktree_root
+    if not worktree_root or not project_root:
+        raise ValueError("identity has no worktree_root/project_root")
+    worktree_hash = identity.worktree_hash or instance_id_for_path(worktree_root)
+    return runtime_dir_for_paths(
+        project_root=project_root, worktree_hash=worktree_hash, registry_path=registry_path
+    )
+
+
 def _port_to_expected_service(
     port: int,
     *,
@@ -419,6 +656,161 @@ def _collision_checks(
     return findings
 
 
+def _to_start_gate(
+    identity: ProjectIdentityView,
+    docker: di.DockerInspectResult,
+    *,
+    catalog: Mapping[str, Any],
+    ports: Mapping[str, int],
+    is_free: Callable[[int], bool],
+    prior_blocking: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]] = (),
+) -> Tuple[List[Dict[str, Any]], List[Dict[str, Any]]]:
+    """Task Orchestrator fixed-port identity gate (Packet 005): ``(blocking, warnings)``.
+
+    ``prior_blocking`` (findings the caller already holds) only suppresses a
+    duplicate ``start_block_code`` entry, as when this ran inline.
+
+    Start is allowed only when the fixed port is free or proven to serve this
+    project. Runs under ``reserved_port_lock(7890)`` (host-wide, serialized
+    with launchers and ``switch-project``, D7; re-entrant, so a caller already
+    holding it does not deadlock). Fail-closed: any error, including taking the
+    lock, is ``TASK_ORCHESTRATOR_START_BLOCKED_UNKNOWN_OWNER``.
+    """
+    blocking: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
+    warnings: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
+    try:
+        with reserved_port_lock(7890):
+            from .task_orchestrator_identity import (
+                evaluate_fixed_port_state,
+                identity_from_docker_labels,
+            )
+
+            to_spec = (catalog.get("servers") or {}).get("task-orchestrator") or {}
+            to_port = ports.get(
+                str(to_spec.get("port_var") or "TASK_ORCHESTRATOR_HTTP_PORT")
+            ) or int(to_spec.get("default_port_base") or 7890)
+            docker_identity = None
+            if docker.available:
+                from .task_orchestrator_identity import identity_from_container_heuristics
+
+                matches = di.find_containers_for_service(
+                    docker,
+                    service_name="task-orchestrator",
+                    expected_ports=[int(to_port)],
+                    name_hints=["task-orchestrator", "orchestrator"],
+                )
+                for c in matches:
+                    st = di.classify_container_ownership(
+                        c,
+                        project_root=identity.project_root,
+                        workspace_id=identity.workspace_id,
+                        project_id=identity.project_id,
+                        expected_ports=[int(to_port)],
+                        expected_name_substrings=["task-orchestrator"],
+                        project_slug_hints=[
+                            identity.project_id or "",
+                            Path(identity.project_root or "").name,
+                        ],
+                    )
+                    if st in {"MATCH", "WRONG_PROJECT"}:
+                        docker_identity = identity_from_docker_labels(
+                            c.labels or {},
+                            port=int(to_port),
+                            container_name=c.name,
+                        )
+                        break
+                    if st in {"COMPOSE_MATCH", "UNLABELED"} and docker_identity is None:
+                        docker_identity = identity_from_container_heuristics(
+                            container_name=c.name,
+                            labels=c.labels or {},
+                            port=int(to_port),
+                            target_project_root=identity.project_root,
+                            target_project_id=identity.project_id,
+                        )
+                        if st == "COMPOSE_MATCH" or docker_identity.has_project_proof():
+                            break
+            to_eval = evaluate_fixed_port_state(
+                port=int(to_port),
+                target_project_id=identity.project_id,
+                target_project_root=identity.project_root,
+                target_workspace_id=identity.workspace_id,
+                target_instance_id=identity.instance_id,
+                target_worktree_hash=identity.worktree_hash,
+                is_free_fn=is_free,
+                docker_identity=docker_identity,
+                skip_http=True,  # upstream jar has no /info|/health (006R)
+                for_start=True,
+            )
+            for f in to_eval.findings:
+                if f.get("severity") == "FAIL" or f.get("code", "").startswith(
+                    "TASK_ORCHESTRATOR_START_BLOCKED"
+                ):
+                    blocking.append(
+                        {
+                            "code": f.get("code")
+                            or to_eval.start_block_code
+                            or "TASK_ORCHESTRATOR_START_BLOCKED_UNKNOWN_OWNER",
+                            "severity": "FAIL",
+                            "message": f.get("message") or "TO start blocked",
+                            "service": "task-orchestrator",
+                        }
+                    )
+                elif f.get("severity") in {"WARN", "UNKNOWN"}:
+                    warnings.append(
+                        {
+                            "code": f.get("code"),
+                            "severity": f.get("severity"),
+                            "message": f.get("message"),
+                            "service": "task-orchestrator",
+                        }
+                    )
+            if not to_eval.start_allowed and to_eval.start_block_code:
+                if not any(
+                    b.get("code") == to_eval.start_block_code
+                    for b in [*prior_blocking, *blocking]
+                ):
+                    blocking.append(
+                        {
+                            "code": to_eval.start_block_code,
+                            "severity": "FAIL",
+                            "message": (
+                                f"task-orchestrator start blocked "
+                                f"(match={to_eval.match} port={to_port})"
+                            ),
+                            "service": "task-orchestrator",
+                        }
+                    )
+            elif to_eval.start_allowed and to_eval.match == "OK":
+                warnings.append(
+                    {
+                        "code": "TASK_ORCHESTRATOR_START_ALLOWED",
+                        "severity": "INFO",
+                        "message": "task-orchestrator already running for target project",
+                        "service": "task-orchestrator",
+                    }
+                )
+            elif to_eval.start_allowed and to_eval.match == "FREE":
+                warnings.append(
+                    {
+                        "code": "TASK_ORCHESTRATOR_START_ALLOWED",
+                        "severity": "INFO",
+                        "message": f"task-orchestrator fixed port :{to_port} free; start allowed",
+                        "service": "task-orchestrator",
+                    }
+                )
+    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
+        blocking.append(
+            {
+                "code": "TASK_ORCHESTRATOR_START_BLOCKED_UNKNOWN_OWNER",
+                "severity": "FAIL",
+                "message": f"TO identity preflight failed: {exc}",
+                "service": "task-orchestrator",
+            }
+        )
+
+    return blocking, warnings

+def _resolve_conport_tenant(
+    identity: ProjectIdentityView,
+    envrc_values: Mapping[str, str],
+    cmd_runner: Optional[Callable[..., Any]],
+) -> Tuple[Optional[ct.TenantCredentials], List[Dict[str, Any]]]:
+    """D5 database phase for this start; ``(None, [FAIL])`` when conport must not start."""
+    try:
+        creds = ct.resolve_tenant(
+            identity,
+            envrc_values=envrc_values,
+            psql=ct.default_psql_runner(runner=cmd_runner),
+        )
+    except ct.TenantError as exc:
+        return None, [{"code": exc.code, "severity": "FAIL", "message": exc.detail or exc.code}]
+    except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
+        return None, [
+            {
+                "code": "CONPORT_TENANT_PROVISION_FAILED",
+                "severity": "FAIL",
+                "message": f"tenant resolution failed ({ct.os_error_detail(exc)})",
+            }
+        ]
+    if creds is None:  # only with provision=False, which a start never passes
+        return None, []
+    if creds.source == "legacy_shared":
+        finding = {
+            "code": "CONPORT_LEGACY_SHARED_STORE",
+            "severity": "WARN",
+            "message": (
+                f"conport uses the shared {ct.SHARED_DB} store ({creds.evidence}); "
+                "migration to a project tenant is M2"
+            ),
+        }
+    else:
+        finding = {
+            "code": "CONPORT_TENANT_SOURCE",
+            "severity": "INFO",
+            "message": f"conport tenant source: {creds.source}",
+        }
+    return creds, [finding]
+
+
+def _run_tenant_gate(
+    identity: ProjectIdentityView,
+    container_name: str,
+    cmd_runner: Optional[Callable[..., Any]],
+    *,
+    expected: bool,
+) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
+    """D5 gate phase in this instance's conport container, under ``provision_lock``.
+
+    The owed gate is re-read under the lock, so two starts never adopt at
+    once and a start whose gate another start finished skips it. FAIL keeps
+    the gate owed for the next start (never re-provisions). ``expected`` is
+    False for the already-running retry, which stays silent when nothing is
+    owed. Lock order: instance/reserved locks outside, ``provision_lock``
+    inside; no registry lock is taken while it is held.
+    """
+    project_id = str(identity.project_id or "")
+    if not project_id:
+        return []
+    try:
+        path = ct.secrets_path(project_id)
+    except ValueError:
+        return []
+    failed = False  # explicit: an empty detail must never read as success
+    detail = ""
+    try:
+        if not expected and ct.pending_gate_credentials(project_id) is None:
+            return []
+        with provision_lock(project_id):
+            pending = ct.pending_gate_credentials(project_id)
+            if pending is None:
+                return [
+                    {
+                        "code": "CONPORT_TENANT_GATE_ADOPTED",
+                        "severity": "INFO",
+                        "message": "migration gate already completed by another start",
+                    }
+                ]
+            ct.adopt_with_gate(container_name, pending, runner=cmd_runner)
+            ct.mark_gate_done(project_id)
+    except ct.TenantError as exc:
+        if exc.code != "CONPORT_TENANT_GATE_FAILED":
+            return [{"code": exc.code, "severity": "FAIL", "message": exc.detail or exc.code}]
+        failed = True
+        detail = exc.detail or "migration gate failed"
+    except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
+        failed = True
+        detail = f"migration gate could not run ({ct.os_error_detail(exc)})"
+    if failed:
+        return [
+            {
+                "code": "CONPORT_TENANT_GATE_FAILED",
+                "severity": "FAIL",
+                "message": (
+                    f"{detail}; the tenant database stays and the next start retries "
+                    f"the gate (never re-provisions); secrets file {path}"
+                ),
+            }
+        ]
+    return [
+        {
+            "code": "CONPORT_TENANT_GATE_ADOPTED",
+            "severity": "INFO",
+            "message": f"migration gate adopted and verified the tenant schema in {container_name}",
+        }
+    ]
+
+

+def _release_stopped_state(
+    *,
+    selected: Sequence[str],
+    worktree_root: str,
+    full_stop: bool,
+    runtime_dir: Path,
+) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
+    """After a successful ``stop`` (called under the instance lock): release leases.
+
+    A full stop (no ``--services``) also removes the worktree's runtime dir unless
+    launchers are still attached to it; a partial one keeps it. Problems become
+    warnings: the containers are already stopped, so this bookkeeping never fails
+    the stop.
+    """
+    from .reap import live_attachments  # reap imports this module
+
+    warnings: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
+
+    def warn(code: str, message: str) -> None:
+        warnings.append({"code": code, "severity": "WARN", "message": message, "service": None})
+
+    def release(leases: PortLeaseRegistry) -> None:
+        for svc in selected:
+            leases.mark_released(service=svc, worktree_root=worktree_root)
+
+    error = save_state_locked(PortLeaseRegistry, default_lease_registry_path(), release)
+    if error:
+        warn("STOP_STATE_RELEASE_INCOMPLETE", f"port leases not released: {error}")
+    if full_stop:
+        live = live_attachments(runtime_dir)
+        if live > 0:
+            warn(
+                "RUNTIME_DIR_KEPT_LIVE_LAUNCHERS",
+                f"{live} launcher(s) still attached; kept runtime dir {runtime_dir}",
+            )
+            return warnings
+        try:
+            shutil.rmtree(runtime_dir)
+        except FileNotFoundError:
+            pass
+        except OSError as exc:
+            warn(
+                "STOP_STATE_RELEASE_INCOMPLETE",
+                f"runtime dir {runtime_dir} not removed: {exc}",
+            )
+    return warnings
+

diff --git a/src/dopemux/mcp/locks.py b/src/dopemux/mcp/locks.py
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..560c59492
--- /dev/null
+++ b/src/dopemux/mcp/locks.py
@@ -0,0 +1,376 @@
+"""Advisory file locks for MCP lifecycle state.
+
+Every lock is an exclusive ``fcntl.flock`` on a small file, released when the
+context manager exits (including on exceptions). Lock files live beside the
+state they guard, so a test that points ``DOPEMUX_MCP_RUNTIME_REGISTRY`` at a
+tmp dir never touches ``~/.dopemux``.
+
+Lock order when nested (never the reverse):
+
+* ``instance_lock``      outside ``registry_lock``
+* ``reserved_port_lock`` outside ``registry_lock``
+* ``instance_lock``      outside ``provision_lock`` (a launcher start provisions
+  the ConPort tenant inside its instance lock; ``provision_lock`` never wraps
+  another lock)
+
+``flock`` is per open file description, so taking ``registry_lock`` again in
+the same process while already holding it deadlocks. Never nest it with
+``locked_reload_and_save``, which takes it itself.
+
+``instance_lock(runtime_dir, blocking=False)`` never waits: it raises
+``LockBusy`` when another holder has the lock (automatic reaps skip that
+instance instead of stalling past their deadline). ``registry_lock(path,
+wait_s=N)`` retries a non-blocking attempt every 50 ms for at most ``N``
+seconds, then raises ``LockBusy``; ``save_state_locked``/``save_states_locked``
+pass that through (only automatic reaps use it; every other caller blocks).
+
+``reserved_port_lock`` is the one exception: it is re-entrant within a thread,
+because the launcher and ``switch-project`` hold it around a lifecycle start
+whose own orchestrator preflight takes it again. Other threads and processes
+still wait for it.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import fcntl
+import getpass
+import hashlib
+import importlib
+import os
+import tempfile
+import threading
+import time
+from contextlib import contextmanager
+from pathlib import Path
+from typing import (
+    Any,
+    Callable,
+    ContextManager,
+    Iterator,
+    List,
+    Optional,
+    Sequence,
+    Tuple,
+    Type,
+    TypeVar,
+)
+
+from .runtime_registry import REGISTRY_ENV, default_registry_path
+
+R = TypeVar("R")
+
+_DIR_MODE = 0o700
+_FILE_MODE = 0o600
+
+
+def _mkdir_private(directory: Path) -> None:
+    """Create ``directory`` and any missing parents with mode 0700."""
+    missing = []
+    cur = directory
+    while not cur.exists():
+        missing.append(cur)
+        if cur.parent == cur:
+            break
+        cur = cur.parent
+    for d in reversed(missing):
+        try:
+            os.mkdir(d, _DIR_MODE)
+        except FileExistsError:
+            continue
+        os.chmod(d, _DIR_MODE)  # defeat a restrictive/odd umask
+
+
+_LOCK_ATTEMPTS = 50
+_LOCK_RETRY_SLEEP_S = 0.005
+#: Retry step of a bounded non-blocking wait (``wait_s``).
+_BUSY_RETRY_S = 0.05
+
+
+class LockBusy(RuntimeError):
+    """A non-blocking lock attempt found the lock held by someone else."""
+
+
+@contextmanager
+def _flock(path: Path, *, blocking: bool = True, wait_s: float = 0.0) -> Iterator[None]:
+    """Exclusive ``flock`` on ``path`` that never holds an orphaned inode.
+
+    A holder may delete the lock file (reap removes a whole runtime dir while
+    holding its ``launch.lock``). A waiter that acquired the old inode would then
+    run alongside a newer holder of a freshly created file, so after acquiring the
+    lock the path is re-checked against the open descriptor and the attempt is
+    retried when the file is gone or replaced. With ``blocking=False`` a held
+    lock is retried (``LOCK_NB``) every 50 ms for at most ``wait_s`` seconds
+    (``0``: one attempt), then raises ``LockBusy``.
+    """
+    mode = fcntl.LOCK_EX if blocking else fcntl.LOCK_EX | fcntl.LOCK_NB
+    give_up = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, wait_s)
+    for _ in range(_LOCK_ATTEMPTS):
+        try:
+            _mkdir_private(path.parent)
+            fd = os.open(str(path), os.O_RDWR | os.O_CREAT, _FILE_MODE)
+        except FileNotFoundError:  # parent removed between mkdir and open
+            time.sleep(_LOCK_RETRY_SLEEP_S)
+            continue
+        acquired = False
+        try:
+            while True:
+                try:
+                    fcntl.flock(fd, mode)
+                    break
+                except BlockingIOError:
+                    if time.monotonic() + _BUSY_RETRY_S > give_up:
+                        raise LockBusy(f"{path} is held by another process") from None
+                    time.sleep(_BUSY_RETRY_S)
+            try:
+                held = os.fstat(fd)
+                current = os.stat(path)
+                acquired = (held.st_dev, held.st_ino) == (
+                    current.st_dev,
+                    current.st_ino,
+                )
+            except FileNotFoundError:
+                acquired = False
+            if acquired:
+                try:
+                    yield
+                finally:
+                    fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_UN)
+                return
+            fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_UN)
+        finally:
+            os.close(fd)
+        time.sleep(_LOCK_RETRY_SLEEP_S)
+    raise OSError(f"could not acquire a stable lock on {path}")
+
+
+def _runtime_dir() -> Path:
+    """Directory holding the shared lock files.
+
+    ``DOPEMUX_MCP_RUNTIME_REGISTRY`` always wins. Under pytest (without the
+    allow-home escape hatch shared with the lease registry) this never resolves
+    ``~/.dopemux``: it falls back to a per-uid/worker/pid/test scratch dir, so a
+    test that forgets the override cannot create lock files in the real home.
+    """
+    if os.environ.get(REGISTRY_ENV):
+        return default_registry_path().parent
+    if os.environ.get("PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST") and not os.environ.get(
+        "DOPEMUX_ALLOW_HOME_LEASE_REGISTRY"
+    ):
+        uid = getpass.getuser() or str(
+            os.getuid() if hasattr(os, "getuid") else "nouser"
+        )
+        worker = os.environ.get("PYTEST_XDIST_WORKER") or "gw0"
+        node = os.environ.get("PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST") or "unknown"
+        session = os.environ.get("PYTEST_XDIST_TESTRUNUID") or os.environ.get(
+            "DOPEMUX_PYTEST_SESSION", "session"
+        )
+        digest = hashlib.sha1(f"{node}:{session}".encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()[:10]
+        return (
+            Path(tempfile.gettempdir())
+            / "dopemux-mcp-tests"
+            / uid
+            / worker
+            / str(os.getpid())
+            / digest
+            / "runtime"
+        )
+    return default_registry_path().parent
+
+
+def registry_lock(
+    registry_path: Optional[Path] = None, *, wait_s: Optional[float] = None
+) -> ContextManager[None]:
+    """Guard read-modify-write of a runtime or lease registry.
+
+    The lock file sits next to ``registry_path`` (the file actually being
+    written); without it, next to the default runtime registry. ``wait_s=None``
+    blocks; a number waits at most that long and then raises ``LockBusy``.
+    """
+    base = Path(registry_path).parent if registry_path is not None else _runtime_dir()
+    if wait_s is None:
+        return _flock(base / "registry.lock")
+    return _flock(base / "registry.lock", blocking=False, wait_s=wait_s)
+
+
+_held = threading.local()
+
+
+@contextmanager
+def _reentrant_flock(path: Path) -> Iterator[None]:
+    """``_flock`` that a thread already holding ``path`` re-enters without blocking."""
+    depth = getattr(_held, "depth", None)
+    if depth is None:
+        depth = _held.depth = {}
+    key = str(path)
+    if depth.get(key):
+        depth[key] += 1
+        try:
+            yield
+        finally:
+            depth[key] -= 1
+        return
+    with _flock(path):
+        depth[key] = 1
+        try:
+            yield
+        finally:
+            del depth[key]
+
+
+def reserved_port_lock(port: int) -> ContextManager[None]:
+    """Serialize starts that contend for one reserved singleton port (re-entrant per thread)."""
+    return _reentrant_flock(_runtime_dir() / f"reserved-{int(port)}.lock")
+
+
+def instance_lock(runtime_dir: Path, *, blocking: bool = True) -> ContextManager[None]:
+    """Serialize launches of one worktree instance (``<runtime_dir>/launch.lock``).
+
+    ``blocking=False`` raises ``LockBusy`` instead of waiting for another holder.
+    """
+    return _flock(Path(runtime_dir) / "launch.lock", blocking=blocking)
+
+
+def project_dir(project_id: str) -> Path:
+    """Per-project state dir ``<runtime parent>/projects/<project_id>``.
+
+    Shared by ``provision_lock`` and the ConPort tenant secrets file, with the
+    same pytest guard as every other lock path (never ``~/.dopemux`` in tests).
+    """
+    pid = str(project_id or "")
+    if pid in {"", ".", ".."} or "/" in pid or "\\" in pid or "\x00" in pid:
+        raise ValueError(f"unsafe project_id for lock path: {project_id!r}")
+    return _runtime_dir().parent / "projects" / pid
+
+
+def provision_lock(project_id: str) -> ContextManager[None]:
+    """Serialize per-project provisioning (tenants, credentials)."""
+    return _flock(project_dir(project_id) / "provision.lock")
+
+
+def _empty_registry_for(registry_cls: Type[R], path: Path) -> R:
+    module = importlib.import_module(registry_cls.__module__)
+    return registry_cls(  # type: ignore[call-arg]
+        path=path,
+        data=module.empty_registry(),
+        present=True,
+        parse_status="OK",
+    )
+
+
+def _reload_and_save(
+    registry_cls: Type[R], reg_path: Path, mutate: Callable[[R], Any]
+) -> R:
+    """Fresh load, ``mutate``, save; the caller holds ``registry_lock(reg_path)``."""
+    recovered = False
+    fresh: R
+    try:
+        fresh = registry_cls.load(reg_path, create_missing=True)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
+        if getattr(fresh, "parse_status", "OK") == "ERROR":
+            recovered = True
+    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - any load failure means start from empty
+        recovered = True
+    if recovered:
+        fresh = _empty_registry_for(registry_cls, reg_path)
+    fresh.recovered_from_corrupt = recovered  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
+    mutate(fresh)
+    fresh.save()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
+    return fresh
+
+
+def locked_reload_and_save(
+    registry_cls: Type[R],
+    path: Path,
+    mutate: Callable[[R], Any],
+    *,
+    wait_s: Optional[float] = None,
+) -> R:
+    """Under ``registry_lock``: fresh load, ``mutate``, save; return the registry.
+
+    A corrupt or unreadable file is replaced by an empty document and the
+    returned registry carries ``recovered_from_corrupt = True``. Works for
+    ``RuntimeRegistry`` and ``PortLeaseRegistry``. ``wait_s`` bounds the lock
+    wait (``LockBusy``); ``None`` blocks.
+    """
+    reg_path = Path(path)
+    with registry_lock(reg_path, wait_s=wait_s):
+        return _reload_and_save(registry_cls, reg_path, mutate)
+
+
+class _CorruptState(Exception):
+    pass
+
+
+def _save_state(
+    registry_cls: Type[R], reg_path: Path, mutate: Callable[[R], Any]
+) -> Optional[str]:
+    """``save_state_locked`` without taking the lock (the caller holds it)."""
+    if not reg_path.exists():
+        return None
+
+    def guarded(reg: R) -> None:
+        if getattr(reg, "recovered_from_corrupt", False):
+            raise _CorruptState()
+        mutate(reg)
+
+    try:
+        _reload_and_save(registry_cls, reg_path, guarded)
+    except _CorruptState:
+        return f"{reg_path} is unreadable; left as is"
+    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
+        return f"{reg_path}: {exc}"
+    return None
+
+
+def save_state_locked(
+    registry_cls: Type[R],
+    path: Path,
+    mutate: Callable[[R], Any],
+    *,
+    wait_s: Optional[float] = None,
+) -> Optional[str]:
+    """``locked_reload_and_save`` that never replaces a corrupt file.
+
+    Returns ``None`` on success (or when ``path`` does not exist: nothing to
+    update) and an error string otherwise. When the fresh load is corrupt,
+    ``mutate`` is not run and nothing is saved, so the unreadable file stays for a
+    person to inspect. With ``wait_s`` a lock that stays busy raises ``LockBusy``
+    (nothing changed); ``None`` blocks.
+    """
+    reg_path = Path(path)
+    if not reg_path.exists():
+        return None
+    try:
+        with registry_lock(reg_path, wait_s=wait_s):
+            return _save_state(registry_cls, reg_path, mutate)
+    except LockBusy:
+        raise
+    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - a lock that never settled, etc.
+        return f"{reg_path}: {exc}"
+
+
+def save_states_locked(
+    updates: Sequence[Tuple[Any, Path, Callable[[Any], Any]]],
+    *,
+    wait_s: Optional[float] = None,
+) -> List[str]:
+    """``save_state_locked`` for several registries under ONE acquisition of their lock.
+
+    All paths must sit in one directory (one ``registry.lock``), so either every
+    registry is updated or, when the lock stays busy past ``wait_s``, none is
+    (``LockBusy``). Returns the per-registry error strings.
+    """
+    paths = [Path(path) for _cls, path, _mutate in updates]
+    if not paths:
+        return []
+    if len({p.parent.resolve() for p in paths}) != 1:
+        raise ValueError("save_states_locked needs registries in one directory")
+    try:
+        with registry_lock(paths[0], wait_s=wait_s):
+            errors = [
+                _save_state(cls, Path(path), mutate) for cls, path, mutate in updates
+            ]
+    except LockBusy:
+        raise
+    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - a lock that never settled, etc.
+        return [f"{paths[0].parent}: {exc}"]
+    return [e for e in errors if e]

+        # from the old URL shape instead of keeping them next to the command.
+        if is_launcher:
+            for stale in ("url", "env"):
+                if stale in new_entry and stale not in desired:
+                    del new_entry[stale]
+                    if "CATALOG_FIELD_DRIFT" not in change_reasons:
+                        change_reasons.append("CATALOG_FIELD_DRIFT")
+
         # Preserve description if user has one and desired also has one: prefer catalog
         if "description" in desired:
             new_entry["description"] = desired["description"]

diff --git a/src/dopemux/mcp/reap.py b/src/dopemux/mcp/reap.py
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..54907d20d
--- /dev/null
+++ b/src/dopemux/mcp/reap.py
@@ -0,0 +1,970 @@
+"""Reap-only cleanup of idle or removed worktree sidecars (design D8, P-09).
+
+A launcher writes ``<runtime_dir>/attached/<pid>`` while it is connected and
+``<runtime_dir>/last_detach`` when it leaves. An instance, the
+``(dopemux.project_id, dopemux.worktree_hash)`` pair, is reaped when it has zero
+live attachments AND (its worktree path no longer exists OR it has been idle at
+least ``idle_s`` since the last detach). Reaping stops and removes the instance's
+containers by container id, releases its port leases, marks its registry entries
+stopped and removes its runtime dir.
+
+Safety rules (all fail closed):
+
+* Containers are joined to an instance by labels ``dopemux.managed=true``,
+  ``dopemux.project_id``, ``dopemux.worktree_hash`` and ``dopemux.scope=worktree``
+  plus a runtime-registry entry for that pair. Anything else is never touched;
+  ``project`` and ``singleton`` scope (the task-orchestrator, host singletons)
+  are never reaped.
+* Eligibility is decided twice: by ``plan_reap`` and again under the instance lock,
+  after pruning dead attach records (which can stamp a newer ``last_detach``). A
+  candidate that no longer has zero live attachments AND (a missing worktree OR
+  ``idle_s`` since ``last_detach``) is skipped as ``stale``: no docker call, no
+  state change.
+* "Idle" needs a ``last_detach`` file. A runtime dir without one has an unknown
+  idle time and only the ``worktree_missing`` reason can select it. A launcher
+  ended by SIGTERM/SIGHUP detaches through ``finally``; one killed outright leaves
+  a dead attach record, and pruning that record stamps ``last_detach`` with the
+  record's mtime unless a newer one exists.
+* Containers are stopped and removed by id, never through ``docker compose`` and
+  never with ``rm -f``. A container that fails to stop keeps its leases, registry
+  entries and runtime dir. Images are never removed: one image per service and
+  Dockerfile version is shared by every worktree and project.
+* Registry entries and leases are changed only for the services whose containers
+  this reap stopped and removed (the ``dopemux.service`` label), service by service:
+  when one service's container fails, the services whose containers are all gone
+  are still released and the runtime dir stays. The task-orchestrator
+  is a ``project``-scope container, never a candidate, so it is never marked stopped.
+  A pair whose worktree is gone and whose containers are already gone (a reap killed
+  after ``docker rm``) is cleaned by a state-only candidate (no container ids); that
+  candidate also clears entries and leases with an empty service (owned by nobody).
+* Automatic runs are bounded (candidate cap, ``docker stop --time``, command timeout,
+  deadline). Under a deadline each docker call gets ``min(cmd_timeout_s, time left)``;
+  a candidate is skipped (``deadline``) only once no time is left. They never wait:
+  each instance lock is taken non-blocking (a busy instance is skipped, ``locked``)
+  and the initial ``docker ps -a`` is bounded by ``min(cmd_timeout_s, time left)``;
+  a timed-out inspection skips the whole run. The final registry/lease update
+  waits for the registry lock at most ``min(time left, 2 s)``; when it stays busy
+  the candidate is skipped as ``registry_busy`` with its containers already gone,
+  registry, leases and runtime dir untouched, and a later run finishes it (a
+  state-only candidate once the worktree is gone). A run that attempted no
+  candidate (``deadline``/``cap``/``locked``/``stale``), or whose inspection timed
+  out, leaves the ``reap.last`` throttle as it was. The explicit CLI ``--apply``
+  waits for every lock.
+* Automatic reaping (:func:`maybe_reap`: hooks and launcher) honours a kill switch:
+  the file ``<runtime root>/reap.disabled`` or ``DOPEMUX_MCP_REAP`` set to
+  ``0``/``off``/``false``. The explicit CLI ``dopemux mcp reap --apply`` ignores it.
+
+Lock order: ``instance_lock(runtime_dir)`` outside, ``registry_lock`` inside
+(through ``locked_reload_and_save``). Never a daemon.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import contextlib
+import os
+import re
+import shutil
+import time
+from dataclasses import dataclass, field, replace
+from datetime import datetime, timezone
+from pathlib import Path
+from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Literal, Optional, Sequence, Tuple
+
+from . import docker_runtime as dr
+from .docker_inspect import DockerContainerInfo, inspect_all_managed_containers
+from .docker_runtime import run_cmd
+from .lifecycle import runtime_dir_for_paths
+from .locks import (
+    LockBusy,
+    _mkdir_private,
+    _runtime_dir,
+    instance_lock,
+    save_state_locked,
+    save_states_locked,
+)
+from .port_leases import PortLeaseRegistry, default_lease_registry_path
+from .runtime_registry import REGISTRY_ENV, RuntimeRegistry, default_registry_path
+
+__all__ = [
+    "DISABLE_ENV",
+    "DISABLE_FILENAME",
+    "ReapCandidate",
+    "ReapReport",
+    "apply_reap",
+    "attach",
+    "detach",
+    "live_attachments",
+    "maybe_reap",
+    "plan_reap",
+    "reaping_disabled_reason",
+    "runtime_registry_path",
+]
+
+ATTACHED_DIR = "attached"
+LAST_DETACH = "last_detach"
+DISABLE_FILENAME = "reap.disabled"
+DISABLE_ENV = "DOPEMUX_MCP_REAP"
+STATE_FILENAME = "reap.last"
+_OFF_VALUES = {"0", "off", "false"}
+_REAPABLE_SCOPE = "worktree"
+_HOOK_DOCKER_TIMEOUT_S = 10.0  # hooks must not stall a turn on a hung docker
+_REGISTRY_WAIT_S = 2.0  # automatic runs wait at most this long for the registry lock
+_SAFE_HASH = re.compile(r"[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}")
+
+Reason = Literal["worktree_missing", "idle"]
+
+
+def _utc_stamp(at: Optional[float] = None) -> str:
+    moment = (
+        datetime.now(timezone.utc)
+        if at is None
+        else datetime.fromtimestamp(at, timezone.utc)
+    )
+    return moment.replace(microsecond=0).isoformat().replace("+00:00", "Z")
+
+
+# --- attachments ------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+
+def attach(runtime_dir: Path) -> Path:
+    """Write ``<runtime_dir>/attached/<pid>`` (ISO-8601 UTC timestamp) for this launcher."""
+    record = Path(runtime_dir) / ATTACHED_DIR / str(os.getpid())
+    _mkdir_private(record.parent)
+    record.write_text(f"{_utc_stamp()}\n", encoding="utf-8")
+    return record
+
+
+def detach(path: Path) -> None:
+    """Remove an attach record and stamp ``last_detach`` in the runtime dir.
+
+    An already-removed record is fine. A runtime dir that was reaped meanwhile is
+    not recreated.
+    """
+    record = Path(path)
+    with contextlib.suppress(FileNotFoundError):
+        record.unlink()
+    runtime_dir = record.parent.parent
+    if not runtime_dir.is_dir():
+        return
+    tmp = runtime_dir / f".{LAST_DETACH}.{os.getpid()}.tmp"
+    try:
+        tmp.write_text(f"{_utc_stamp()}\n", encoding="utf-8")
+        os.replace(tmp, runtime_dir / LAST_DETACH)
+    except OSError:
+        with contextlib.suppress(OSError):
+            tmp.unlink()
+
+
+def _pid_alive(pid: int) -> bool:
+    try:
+        os.kill(pid, 0)
+    except ProcessLookupError:
+        return False
+    except PermissionError:
+        return True  # exists, owned by someone else
+    except OSError:
+        return True  # unknown: fail closed
+    return True
+
+
+def _stamp_last_detach(runtime_dir: Path, at: float) -> None:
+    """Set ``last_detach`` to ``at`` unless it already records a later detach."""
+    marker = runtime_dir / LAST_DETACH
+    try:
+        if marker.stat().st_mtime >= at:
+            return
+    except FileNotFoundError:
+        pass
+    except OSError:
+        return
+    tmp = runtime_dir / f".{LAST_DETACH}.{os.getpid()}.tmp"
+    try:
+        tmp.write_text(f"{_utc_stamp(at)}\n", encoding="utf-8")
+        os.utime(tmp, (at, at))
+        os.replace(tmp, marker)
+    except OSError:
+        with contextlib.suppress(OSError):
+            tmp.unlink()
+
+
+def live_attachments(runtime_dir: Path, *, prune: bool = True) -> int:
+    """Number of attach records whose pid is alive.
+
+    The pid is the file name (older records hold the pid as content, newer ones a
+    timestamp; both work). Records of dead pids are removed when ``prune`` is true,
+    and the newest pruned record's mtime becomes ``last_detach`` when that file is
+    absent or older (a launcher killed with SIGKILL never detached). Planning
+    passes ``prune=False`` so a dry run never changes anything. An unreadable
+    ``attached`` dir counts as attached.
+    """
+    attached = Path(runtime_dir) / ATTACHED_DIR
+    try:
+        entries = list(attached.iterdir())
+    except FileNotFoundError:
+        return 0
+    except OSError:
+        return 1
+    live = 0
+    newest_dead: Optional[float] = None
+    for entry in entries:
+        name = entry.name
+        if not name.isdigit() or int(name) <= 0:
+            continue
+        if _pid_alive(int(name)):
+            live += 1
+        elif prune:
+            try:
+                mtime = entry.stat().st_mtime
+                entry.unlink()
+            except OSError:
+                continue
+            newest_dead = mtime if newest_dead is None else max(newest_dead, mtime)
+    if newest_dead is not None:
+        _stamp_last_detach(Path(runtime_dir), newest_dead)
+    return live
+
+
+# --- kill switch and paths --------------------------------------------------------
+
+
+def runtime_registry_path() -> Path:
+    """The runtime registry, redirected like the lock files under pytest."""
+    if os.environ.get(REGISTRY_ENV):
+        return default_registry_path()
+    return _runtime_dir() / "instances.json"
+
+
+def reaping_disabled_reason() -> Optional[str]:
+    """Why automatic reaping is switched off, or ``None`` when it is allowed."""
+    raw = os.environ.get(DISABLE_ENV)
+    if raw is not None and raw.strip().lower() in _OFF_VALUES:
+        return f"{DISABLE_ENV}={raw.strip()}"
+    marker = _runtime_dir() / DISABLE_FILENAME
+    try:
+        if marker.exists():
+            return f"{marker} exists"
+    except OSError as exc:
+        return f"{marker} unreadable ({exc})"
+    return None
+
+
+# --- plan -------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+
+@dataclass
+class ReapCandidate:
+    project_id: str
+    worktree_hash: str
+    worktree_root: str
+    runtime_dir: Path
+    container_ids: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
+    reason: Reason = "worktree_missing"
+    services: List[str] = field(
+        default_factory=list
+    )  # dopemux.service of those containers
+    #: ``container id -> dopemux.service`` (for releasing state service by service).
+    container_services: Dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)
+
+    @property
+    def state_only(self) -> bool:
+        """No container left: only leases, registry entries and the runtime dir."""
+        return not self.container_ids
+
+    def to_dict(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
+        return {
+            "project_id": self.project_id,
+            "worktree_hash": self.worktree_hash,
+            "worktree_root": self.worktree_root,
+            "runtime_dir": str(self.runtime_dir),
+            "container_ids": list(self.container_ids),
+            "reason": self.reason,
+            "services": list(self.services),
+        }
+
+
+@dataclass
+class ReapReport:
+    reaped: List[ReapCandidate] = field(default_factory=list)
+    errors: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
+    skipped: List[ReapCandidate] = field(default_factory=list)
+    skip_reasons: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)  # parallel to ``skipped``
+
+    def skip(self, cand: ReapCandidate, reason: str) -> None:
+        """``reason``: ``attached`` (a launcher attached first), ``cap``, ``deadline``,
+        ``locked`` (an automatic run found the instance lock busy), ``stale`` (no
+        longer eligible under the lock) or ``registry_busy`` (containers removed, the
+        registry lock stayed busy: a later run finishes the state)."""
+        self.skipped.append(cand)
+        self.skip_reasons.append(reason)
+
+    def to_dict(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
+        return {
+            "reaped": [c.to_dict() for c in self.reaped],
+            "skipped": [
+                {**c.to_dict(), "skip_reason": r}
+                for c, r in zip(self.skipped, self.skip_reasons)
+            ],
+            "errors": list(self.errors),
+        }
+
+
+def _worktree_gone(root: str, exists: Callable[[Path], bool]) -> bool:
+    try:
+        return not exists(Path(root))
+    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - unreadable means unknown, never "missing"
+        return False
+
+
+_ORCHESTRATOR = "task-orchestrator"
+
+
+def _entry_for(
+    registry: RuntimeRegistry, project_id: str, worktree_hash: str
+) -> Optional[Tuple[str, str, List[Dict[str, Any]]]]:
+    """``(worktree_root, project_root, entries)`` of the pair's worktree-scope entries."""
+    roots = set()
+    project_root = ""
+    entries: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
+    for inst in registry.instances():
+        if (
+            inst.get("project_id") == project_id
+            and inst.get("worktree_hash") == worktree_hash
+            and inst.get("scope") == _REAPABLE_SCOPE
+            and inst.get("worktree_root")
+        ):
+            roots.add(str(inst["worktree_root"]))
+            project_root = str(inst.get("project_root") or inst["worktree_root"])
+            entries.append(inst)
+    if len(roots) != 1:
+        return None
+    return next(iter(roots)), project_root, entries
+
+
+def _still_eligible(
+    cand: "ReapCandidate", *, now: float, idle_s: float, exists: Callable[[Path], bool]
+) -> bool:
+    """The plan's predicate on current state (live attachments checked by the caller)."""
+    if _worktree_gone(cand.worktree_root, exists):
+        return True
+    age = _last_detach_age(cand.runtime_dir, now)
+    return age is not None and age >= idle_s
+
+
+def _last_detach_age(runtime_dir: Path, now: float) -> Optional[float]:
+    try:
+        return now - (runtime_dir / LAST_DETACH).stat().st_mtime
+    except OSError:
+        return None
+
+
+def _has_active_leases(
+    leases: Optional[PortLeaseRegistry], worktree_root: str, project_id: str
+) -> bool:
+    if leases is None or leases.parse_status != "OK":
+        return False
+    root = worktree_root.rstrip("/")
+    return any(
+        str(lease.get("worktree_root") or "").rstrip("/") == root
+        and (lease.get("scope") or _REAPABLE_SCOPE) == _REAPABLE_SCOPE
+        and lease.get("service") != _ORCHESTRATOR
+        and (not lease.get("project_id") or lease["project_id"] == project_id)
+        for lease in leases.active_leases()
+    )
+
+
+def plan_reap(
+    *,
+    registry: RuntimeRegistry,
+    containers: Sequence[DockerContainerInfo],
+    now: float,
+    idle_s: float = 1200,
+    exists: Callable[[Path], bool] = Path.exists,
+    lease_registry: Optional[PortLeaseRegistry] = None,
+) -> List[ReapCandidate]:
+    """Instances to reap. Read-only; an unreadable registry yields ``[]``.
+
+    Besides container candidates, a registry pair whose worktree is gone and which
+    has no container at all but still has unfinished state (a running entry, active
+    leases or a runtime dir) yields a state-only candidate, so a reap that died
+    after ``docker rm`` is finished by the next run.
+    """
+    if registry.parse_status != "OK":
+        return []
+
+    groups: Dict[Tuple[str, str], List[DockerContainerInfo]] = {}
+    seen_pairs = set()
+    for c in containers:
+        labels = c.labels or {}
+        project_id = labels.get("dopemux.project_id") or ""
+        worktree_hash = labels.get("dopemux.worktree_hash") or ""
+        if labels.get(dr.LABEL_MANAGED) == "true" and project_id and worktree_hash:
+            seen_pairs.add((project_id, worktree_hash))
+        if (
+            labels.get(dr.LABEL_MANAGED) != "true"
+            or labels.get("dopemux.scope") != _REAPABLE_SCOPE
+            or not project_id
+            or not _SAFE_HASH.fullmatch(worktree_hash)
+            or not c.id
+        ):
+            continue
+        groups.setdefault((project_id, worktree_hash), []).append(c)
+
+    candidates: List[ReapCandidate] = []
+    for (project_id, worktree_hash), members in groups.items():
+        entry = _entry_for(registry, project_id, worktree_hash)
+        if entry is None:
+            continue
+        worktree_root, project_root, _ = entry
+        runtime_dir = runtime_dir_for_paths(
+            project_root=project_root,
+            worktree_hash=worktree_hash,
+            registry_path=registry.path,
+        )
+        if live_attachments(runtime_dir, prune=False) > 0:
+            continue
+        reason: Optional[Reason] = None
+        if _worktree_gone(worktree_root, exists):
+            reason = "worktree_missing"
+        else:
+            age = _last_detach_age(runtime_dir, now)
+            if age is not None and age >= idle_s:
+                reason = "idle"
+        if reason is None:
+            continue
+        candidates.append(
+            ReapCandidate(
+                project_id=project_id,
+                worktree_hash=worktree_hash,
+                worktree_root=worktree_root,
+                runtime_dir=runtime_dir,
+                container_ids=sorted(c.id for c in members),
+                reason=reason,
+                services=sorted(
+                    {(c.labels or {}).get("dopemux.service") or "" for c in members}
+                    - {""}
+                ),
+                container_services={
+                    c.id: (c.labels or {}).get("dopemux.service") or "" for c in members
+                },
+            )
+        )
+
+    pairs = {
+        (str(i.get("project_id") or ""), str(i.get("worktree_hash") or ""))
+        for i in registry.instances()
+        if i.get("scope") == _REAPABLE_SCOPE
+    }
+    for project_id, worktree_hash in sorted(pairs):
+        if (
+            not project_id
+            or not _SAFE_HASH.fullmatch(worktree_hash)
+            or (project_id, worktree_hash) in seen_pairs
+        ):
+            continue
+        entry = _entry_for(registry, project_id, worktree_hash)
+        if entry is None:
+            continue
+        worktree_root, project_root, entries = entry
+        if not _worktree_gone(worktree_root, exists):
+            continue
+        runtime_dir = runtime_dir_for_paths(
+            project_root=project_root,
+            worktree_hash=worktree_hash,
+            registry_path=registry.path,
+        )
+        if live_attachments(runtime_dir, prune=False) > 0:
+            continue
+        unfinished = (
+            any(
+                e.get("status") != "stopped" and e.get("service") != _ORCHESTRATOR
+                for e in entries
+            )
+            or _has_active_leases(lease_registry, worktree_root, project_id)
+            or runtime_dir.exists()
+        )
+        if unfinished:
+            candidates.append(
+                ReapCandidate(
+                    project_id=project_id,
+                    worktree_hash=worktree_hash,
+                    worktree_root=worktree_root,
+                    runtime_dir=runtime_dir,
+                    container_ids=[],
+                    reason="worktree_missing",
+                    services=[],
+                )
+            )
+    return candidates
+
+
+# --- apply ------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+
+def _owned_by(service: Optional[str], cand: ReapCandidate) -> bool:
+    """Whether this reap is responsible for ``service`` of the candidate's pair.
+
+    An empty service belongs to nobody: only a state-only candidate (worktree
+    gone, no container left) clears it, so it cannot keep the pair alive forever.
+    """
+    if not service:
+        return cand.state_only
+    if cand.state_only:
+        return service != _ORCHESTRATOR  # never the reserved orchestrator
+    return service in cand.services
+
+
+def _release_leases(reg: PortLeaseRegistry, cand: ReapCandidate) -> None:
+    root = cand.worktree_root.rstrip("/")
+    for lease in reg.leases():
+        if lease.get("status") != "active":
+            continue
+        if str(lease.get("worktree_root") or "").rstrip("/") != root:
+            continue
+        if (lease.get("scope") or _REAPABLE_SCOPE) != _REAPABLE_SCOPE:
+            continue
+        if lease.get("project_id") and lease["project_id"] != cand.project_id:
+            continue
+        if not _owned_by(lease.get("service"), cand):
+            continue
+        if lease.get("lease_id"):
+            reg.mark_released(lease_id=lease["lease_id"])
+        elif lease.get("service"):
+            reg.mark_released(
+                service=lease["service"], worktree_root=cand.worktree_root
+            )
+        else:  # no id and no service: nothing else can address it
+            lease["status"] = "released"
+            lease["updated_at"] = _utc_stamp()
+
+
+def _mark_instances_stopped(reg: RuntimeRegistry, cand: ReapCandidate) -> None:
+    owned = [
+        inst
+        for inst in reg.instances()
+        if inst.get("project_id") == cand.project_id
+        and inst.get("worktree_hash") == cand.worktree_hash
+        and inst.get("scope") == _REAPABLE_SCOPE
+        and _owned_by(inst.get("service"), cand)
+    ]
+    for service in sorted(
+        {str(inst["service"]) for inst in owned if inst.get("service")}
+    ):
+        reg.mark_stopped(
+            project_id=cand.project_id,
+            service=service,
+            worktree_root=cand.worktree_root,
+        )
+    for inst in owned:
+        if not inst.get(
+            "service"
+        ):  # mark_stopped(service="") would match every service
+            inst["status"] = "stopped"
+            inst["updated_at"] = _utc_stamp()
+
+
+def _runtime_dir_is_ours(cand: ReapCandidate, registry_path: Path) -> bool:
+    rt = Path(cand.runtime_dir)
+    return (
+        bool(_SAFE_HASH.fullmatch(cand.worktree_hash))
+        and rt.name.endswith(f"-{cand.worktree_hash}")
+        and rt.parent.resolve() == registry_path.parent.resolve()
+    )
+
+
+def _already_gone(result: Any) -> bool:
+    return "no such container" in (result.stderr or "").lower()
+
+
+def _stop_and_remove(
+    cand: ReapCandidate,
+    runner: Callable[..., Any],
+    *,
+    stop_time_s: Optional[int],
+    call_timeout: Callable[[], Optional[float]] = lambda: None,
+    out_of_time: Callable[[], bool] = lambda: False,
+) -> Tuple[List[str], List[str]]:
+    """``(errors, ids of containers now gone)``; ``call_timeout()`` bounds each docker call."""
+
+    def kwargs() -> Dict[str, Any]:
+        timeout = call_timeout()
+        return {} if timeout is None else {"timeout": timeout}
+
+    stop_cmd = ["docker", "stop"] + (
+        [] if stop_time_s is None else ["--time", str(stop_time_s)]
+    )
+    errors: List[str] = []
+    gone: List[str] = []
+    for cid in cand.container_ids:
+        if out_of_time():
+            errors.append(
+                f"{cid}: deadline reached before docker call; resumes next run"
+            )
+            break
+        stop = runner([*stop_cmd, cid], **kwargs())
+        if stop.exit_code != 0 and not _already_gone(stop):
+            errors.append(
+                f"{cid}: docker stop failed ({stop.exit_code}): {stop.stderr.strip()[:200]}"
+            )
+            continue  # never remove (let alone force-remove) what did not stop
+        if out_of_time():
+            errors.append(f"{cid}: deadline reached before docker rm; resumes next run")
+            break
+        rm = runner(["docker", "rm", cid], **kwargs())
+        if rm.exit_code != 0 and not _already_gone(rm):
+            errors.append(
+                f"{cid}: docker rm failed ({rm.exit_code}): {rm.stderr.strip()[:200]}"
+            )
+            continue
+        gone.append(cid)
+    return errors, gone
+
+
+def _services_gone(cand: ReapCandidate, gone: Sequence[str]) -> List[str]:
+    """Services of ``cand`` all of whose containers are in ``gone``."""
+    removed = set(gone)
+    return [
+        svc
+        for svc in cand.services
+        if all(
+            cid in removed
+            for cid, owner in cand.container_services.items()
+            if owner == svc
+        )
+        and any(owner == svc for owner in cand.container_services.values())
+    ]
+
+
+def _release_state(
+    cand: ReapCandidate,
+    lease_path: Path,
+    registry_path: Path,
+    report: ReapReport,
+    label: str,
+    *,
+    wait_s: Optional[float] = None,
+) -> None:
+    """Release ``cand``'s leases and mark its registry entries stopped (registry lock inside).
+
+    Both registries in one directory share one ``registry.lock`` and are updated
+    under one acquisition, so a lock that stays busy past ``wait_s`` raises
+    ``LockBusy`` with neither changed. ``wait_s=None`` blocks (the CLI).
+    """
+    updates: List[Tuple[Any, Path, Callable[[Any], Any]]] = [
+        (PortLeaseRegistry, lease_path, lambda reg: _release_leases(reg, cand)),
+        (
+            RuntimeRegistry,
+            registry_path,
+            lambda reg: _mark_instances_stopped(reg, cand),
+        ),
+    ]
+    if Path(lease_path).parent.resolve() == Path(registry_path).parent.resolve():
+        errors = save_states_locked(updates, wait_s=wait_s)
+    else:  # separate directories (overridden paths): one lock each
+        errors = [
+            e
+            for e in (save_state_locked(c, p, m, wait_s=wait_s) for c, p, m in updates)
+            if e
+        ]
+    report.errors.extend(f"{label}: {error}" for error in errors)
+
+
+def _reap_one(
+    cand: ReapCandidate,
+    runner: Callable[..., Any],
+    lease_path: Path,
+    registry_path: Path,
+    report: ReapReport,
+    *,
+    stop_time_s: Optional[int],
+    call_timeout: Callable[[], Optional[float]] = lambda: None,
+    out_of_time: Callable[[], bool] = lambda: False,
+    blocking_lock: bool = True,
+    now: Callable[[], float] = time.time,
+    idle_s: float = 1200,
+    exists: Callable[[Path], bool] = Path.exists,
+    registry_wait: Optional[Callable[[], float]] = None,
+) -> None:
+    label = f"{cand.project_id}/{cand.worktree_hash}"
+    if not _runtime_dir_is_ours(cand, registry_path):
+        report.errors.append(
+            f"{label}: runtime dir {cand.runtime_dir} is outside {registry_path.parent}"
+        )
+        return
+    with contextlib.ExitStack() as stack:
+        try:
+            stack.enter_context(instance_lock(cand.runtime_dir, blocking=blocking_lock))
+        except LockBusy:
+            report.skip(cand, "locked")  # a launcher or stop holds it: next run
+            return
+        if live_attachments(cand.runtime_dir) > 0:
+            report.skip(cand, "attached")
+            return
+        if not _still_eligible(cand, now=now(), idle_s=idle_s, exists=exists):
+            report.skip(
+                cand, "stale"
+            )  # a launcher came and went, or the worktree is back
+            return
+        docker_errors, gone = _stop_and_remove(
+            cand,
+            runner,
+            stop_time_s=stop_time_s,
+            call_timeout=call_timeout,
+            out_of_time=out_of_time,
+        )
+        if docker_errors:
+            report.errors.extend(f"{label}: {e}" for e in docker_errors)
+            # Some containers may still run: keep their leases, entries and the dir,
+            # but release every service whose containers are all gone now.
+            done = _services_gone(cand, gone)
+            if done:
+                try:
+                    _release_state(
+                        replace(cand, services=done),
+                        lease_path,
+                        registry_path,
+                        report,
+                        label,
+                        wait_s=None if registry_wait is None else registry_wait(),
+                    )
+                except LockBusy:
+                    report.errors.append(
+                        f"{label}: registry busy; the state of {', '.join(done)} "
+                        "is released by a later run"
+                    )
+            return
+        try:
+            _release_state(
+                cand,
+                lease_path,
+                registry_path,
+                report,
+                label,
+                wait_s=None if registry_wait is None else registry_wait(),
+            )
+        except LockBusy:
+            # Containers are gone; registry, leases and the runtime dir stay for a
+            # later run (a state-only candidate once the worktree is gone).
+            report.skip(cand, "registry_busy")
+            return
+        try:
+            shutil.rmtree(cand.runtime_dir)
+        except FileNotFoundError:
+            pass
+        except OSError as exc:
+            report.errors.append(f"{label}: could not remove {cand.runtime_dir}: {exc}")
+            return
+        report.reaped.append(cand)
+
+
+def apply_reap(
+    candidates: Sequence[ReapCandidate],
+    *,
+    runner: Callable[..., Any] = run_cmd,
+    lease_path: Optional[Path] = None,
+    registry_path: Optional[Path] = None,
+    max_candidates: Optional[int] = None,
+    stop_time_s: Optional[int] = None,
+    cmd_timeout_s: Optional[float] = None,
+    deadline: Optional[float] = None,
+    clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic,
+    blocking_locks: bool = True,
+    now: Callable[[], float] = time.time,
+    idle_s: float = 1200,
+    exists: Callable[[Path], bool] = Path.exists,
+) -> ReapReport:
+    """Reap each candidate under its instance lock; failures are recorded, never raised.
+
+    Under the lock the plan's predicate is evaluated again on current state
+    (``now``, ``idle_s``, ``exists`` as for ``plan_reap``); a candidate that no
+    longer qualifies is skipped as ``stale``. ``blocking_locks=False`` (automatic
+    runs) skips a candidate whose instance lock is busy (``locked``) and waits for
+    the registry lock at most ``min(time left, 2 s)`` (``registry_busy``).
+
+    ``max_candidates`` caps the work of one call, ``stop_time_s`` becomes
+    ``docker stop --time N``, ``cmd_timeout_s`` is the per-command timeout and
+    ``deadline`` an absolute ``clock()`` (monotonic) value. Under a deadline each
+    docker call is bounded by ``min(cmd_timeout_s, time left)``, so a timeout longer
+    than the time left never skips a candidate by itself; once no time is left the
+    remaining candidates are reported under ``skipped`` (reasons ``cap`` /
+    ``deadline``) and wait for the next run. All default to unbounded (the explicit
+    CLI).
+    """
+    leases = (
+        Path(lease_path) if lease_path is not None else default_lease_registry_path()
+    )
+    registry = (
+        Path(registry_path) if registry_path is not None else runtime_registry_path()
+    )
+    report = ReapReport()
+    limit = len(candidates) if max_candidates is None else max(0, max_candidates)
+
+    def time_left() -> Optional[float]:
+        return None if deadline is None else deadline - clock()
+
+    def out_of_time() -> bool:
+        left = time_left()
+        return left is not None and left <= 0
+
+    def call_timeout() -> Optional[float]:
+        left = time_left()
+        if left is None:
+            return cmd_timeout_s
+        return left if cmd_timeout_s is None else min(cmd_timeout_s, left)
+
+    def registry_wait() -> float:
+        left = time_left()
+        return (
+            _REGISTRY_WAIT_S if left is None else max(0.0, min(_REGISTRY_WAIT_S, left))
+        )
+
+    for index, cand in enumerate(candidates):
+        if index >= limit:
+            report.skip(cand, "cap")
+            continue
+        if out_of_time():
+            report.skip(cand, "deadline")
+            continue
+        try:
+            _reap_one(
+                cand,
+                runner,
+                leases,
+                registry,
+                report,
+                stop_time_s=stop_time_s,
+                call_timeout=call_timeout,
+                out_of_time=out_of_time,
+                blocking_lock=blocking_locks,
+                now=now,
+                idle_s=idle_s,
+                exists=exists,
+                registry_wait=None if blocking_locks else registry_wait,
+            )
+        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - one bad candidate stops no other
+            report.errors.append(f"{cand.project_id}/{cand.worktree_hash}: {exc}")
+    return report
+
+
+# --- automatic entry point --------------------------------------------------------
+
+
+def maybe_reap(
+    *,
+    throttle_s: float = 600,
+    state_file: Optional[Path] = None,
+    now: Callable[[], float] = time.time,
+    idle_s: float = 1200,
+    inspector: Optional[Callable[..., Any]] = None,
+    runner: Optional[Callable[..., Any]] = None,
+    max_candidates: Optional[int] = 3,
+    stop_time_s: Optional[int] = 5,
+    cmd_timeout_s: Optional[float] = 20.0,
+    deadline_s: Optional[float] = None,
+    monotonic: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic,
+) -> Optional[ReapReport]:
+    """Throttled, bounded plan-and-apply for hooks and launchers; ``None`` if it did not run.
+
+    Does nothing when automatic reaping is disabled (see module docstring) or when
+    ``state_file`` (default ``<runtime root>/reap.last``) was touched within
+    ``throttle_s`` seconds. Docker is only asked when the runtime registry holds
+    instances; an unreachable docker reaps nothing. Defaults suit hooks: at most 3
+    candidates, ``docker stop --time 5``, 20 s per command; the Stop hook adds a 60 s
+    deadline. A launcher passes 1 candidate, ``--time 2``, 3 s per command and an 8 s
+    deadline because it runs inside Claude's connect budget; SessionEnd passes 1 / 3 /
+    10 and a 45 s deadline.
+
+    ``state_file`` is touched before any work (so concurrent hooks do not all run);
+    when candidates existed but none was attempted (deadline, cap, or a busy
+    instance lock), or the docker inspection timed out, the touch is undone, so a
+    run that did nothing never consumes the throttle. Instance locks are taken
+    non-blocking and the inspection is bounded by ``min(cmd_timeout_s, time left)``.
+    """
+    if reaping_disabled_reason() is not None:
+        return None
+    deadline = None if deadline_s is None else monotonic() + deadline_s
+    state = (
+        Path(state_file) if state_file is not None else _runtime_dir() / STATE_FILENAME
+    )
+    current = now()
+    if throttle_s > 0:
+        try:
+            if current - state.stat().st_mtime < throttle_s:
+                return None
+        except OSError:
+            pass
+    try:
+        previous: Optional[float] = state.stat().st_mtime
+    except OSError:
+        previous = None
+    with contextlib.suppress(OSError):
+        state.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+        state.touch()
+        os.utime(state, (current, current))
+
+    report = ReapReport()
+    registry = RuntimeRegistry.load(runtime_registry_path())
+    if registry.parse_status == "ERROR":
+        report.errors.append(f"{registry.path}: unreadable ({registry.error})")
+        return report
+    if not registry.instances():
+        return report
+    inspect_timeout = min(
+        _HOOK_DOCKER_TIMEOUT_S, cmd_timeout_s or _HOOK_DOCKER_TIMEOUT_S
+    )
+    if deadline is not None:
+        left = deadline - monotonic()
+        if left <= 0:
+            _restore_throttle(state, previous)
+            report.errors.append("deadline reached before docker inspection; skipped")
+            return report
+        inspect_timeout = min(inspect_timeout, left)
+    run_inspect = inspector or inspect_all_managed_containers
+    docker = run_inspect(timeout=inspect_timeout)
+    if not docker.available:
+        timed_out = "timed out" in str(docker.error or "").lower() or (
+            deadline is not None and monotonic() >= deadline
+        )
+        if timed_out:  # a slow docker skips the whole run and touches nothing
+            _restore_throttle(state, previous)
+            report.errors.append(
+                f"docker inspection timed out; skipped ({docker.error})"
+            )
+            return report
+        report.errors.append(f"docker unavailable: {docker.error}")
+        return report
+    candidates = plan_reap(
+        registry=registry,
+        containers=docker.containers,
+        now=current,
+        idle_s=idle_s,
+        lease_registry=PortLeaseRegistry.load(default_lease_registry_path()),
+    )
+    if not candidates:
+        return report
+    report = apply_reap(
+        candidates,
+        runner=runner or run_cmd,
+        max_candidates=max_candidates,
+        stop_time_s=stop_time_s,
+        cmd_timeout_s=cmd_timeout_s,
+        deadline=deadline,
+        clock=monotonic,
+        blocking_locks=False,
+        now=now,
+        idle_s=idle_s,
+    )
+    not_attempted = sum(
+        1 for r in report.skip_reasons if r in {"cap", "deadline", "locked", "stale"}
+    )
+    if not_attempted == len(candidates):
+        _restore_throttle(state, previous)
+    return report
+
+
+def _restore_throttle(state: Path, previous: Optional[float]) -> None:
+    """Undo ``maybe_reap``'s touch of ``state`` (to ``previous``, or remove it)."""
+    with contextlib.suppress(OSError):
+        if previous is None:
+            state.unlink()
+        else:
+            os.utime(state, (previous, previous))

+def sidecar_url(spec: Mapping[str, Any], ports: Mapping[str, int]) -> Optional[str]:
+    """The sidecar endpoint a catalog entry resolves to, given its leased ports.
+
+    Expands `url_template`'s `${VAR:-default}` placeholders from `ports`. Returns
+    None when `ports` lacks the entry's `port_var` or any variable the template
+    names: the template default may be another worktree's port, so callers refuse
+    rather than guess. The catalog, not `.mcp.json`, is the source: a launcher
+    entry carries no URL.
+    """
+    template = spec.get("url_template") or spec.get("url")
+    if not template:
+        raise ValueError("catalog entry has no `url_template`/`url` to resolve a sidecar URL from")
+    needed = {m.group(1) for m in _ENV_PLACEHOLDER.finditer(str(template))}
+    if spec.get("port_var"):
+        needed.add(str(spec["port_var"]))
+    if any(name not in ports for name in needed):
+        return None
+    return expand_url_template(str(template), {k: str(v) for k, v in ports.items()})
+
